Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.


# The document under review (round 1 snapshot)

# bennett-arith-4yg.1 — PPC parity where the low component becomes zero

Council proposal, not a published amendment. Owner: dialect specification;
implementation follow-up: `bennett-arith-4yg`. References are to maintained
[dialect 0.2.12](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md)
§§13.1, 13.3–13.5 and the corresponding
[0.3 draft](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md).

## Exact defect and North Star scope

§13.1, “PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding”, defines canonical finite
pairs by h = binary64 roundTiesToEven(h+l), with l the exact residual.
It then requires the nearest canonical sum, “with ties to the even
low-component significand, using the high significand when the low
component is zero”. Adjacent canonical candidates can both be odd under
that rule. Thus a required result is not uniquely specified for valid
FMA and incoming IEEE conversion inputs. This is a numerical contract
defect, rather than an absent softfloat feature or an ISA operand defect.

§13.1 already determines the flags: only final rounding counts; underflow
requires a tiny rounded total and inexactness. The observed FMA flags 0
and conversion flags 24 are implementation defects independently of the
tie decision. They must become 16 whichever nearest candidate is chosen.

North Star §§3.2, 5.1 requires deterministic materialised numerical
semantics and independent comparison. The missing tie decision obstructs
that contract; it is not a missing North Star machine capability. All
seven formats, full input domains, conversions, FMA and the demo ladder
remain required. §13.3 expressly forbids treating missing numerical
implementation as a supported-format restriction.

## Alternatives and concrete witnesses

Let u = 2^-1074, h = 1+2^-52 (binary64 `3ff0000000000001`). The
canonical inputs a=(h,u), b=(1/2,+0), c=(h/2,+0) have exact fused result
h+u/2. Its adjacent canonical results are L=(h,+0), U=(h,u).
Every finite binary64, hence every pair sum, is an integer multiple of u;
their separation u proves adjacency, and both distances are u/2.
L's published high fallback is odd; U's low significand is 1, also odd.

For conversion, take H with binary64 bits `0030000000000001`.
V=H+u/2 is exactly binary128 bits `3c030000000000001200000000000000`.
The adjacent pairs (H,+0), (H,u) have the same odd/odd problem. H is
normal, so this conversion cannot raise underflow merely because its
unrepresentable residual is tiny. These are two different operation paths
to the same decision, not a speculative rare input.

| Alternative | Consequence |
|---|---|
| Compare low significands for both candidates if either low is nonzero; use high only if both are zero | Zero has even low parity, selecting L in both witnesses. Recommended. |
| Always compare high significands | Both highs are the same here; fails to settle these ties. |
| Always compare low significands | Both lows are zero for adjacent small subnormal totals; needs another rule there. |
| Specify nearest ties toward zero everywhere | Deterministic, but changes existing ties with a unique even candidate unnecessarily. |
| Defer to APFloat or use a 106-bit grid | Violates the explicit exact-value priority and loses admitted values. |

The recommendation includes a deterministic fallback if both candidates
receive the same parity. It does not claim an exhaustive theorem that
such a fallback is unreachable across all canonical high-bin boundaries.
This small added policy keeps the published rule total without making
implementers invent a second tie decision.

## Recommended exact spec diff

In §13.1's finite-canonical-pair paragraph in both dialect tracks, replace:

```diff
-with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high
-significand when the low component is zero,
+with nearest ties resolved as follows. Let L and U be the adjacent
+canonical finite values bracketing the exact result. If either low
+component is nonzero, compare the parities of BOTH low-component
+integer significands, treating a zero significand as even. Otherwise
+compare the high-component integer significands. The integer
+significand is the binary64 significand including the implicit leading
+bit for a normal value and without it for a subnormal; its sign does
+not affect parity. Choose the candidate of even parity when exactly
+one is even; if the parities coincide, choose the candidate whose
+exact sum has smaller absolute value. Existing exact-zero sign and
+overflow rules apply separately. This tie rule applies wherever this
+section requires nearest-even canonical-pair rounding, including
+incoming IEEE conversions and FMA; directed and nearest-away modes
+retain their specified rules,
```

The surrounding sentence should retain its existing NaN/inf and overflow
clauses. No change to §13.5's flags table or the exact-sum conversion
paragraph is needed. Add this explicit normative witness immediately
after that paragraph:

```text
For u=2^-1074 and h=1+2^-52, fma((h,u),(1/2,+0),(h/2,+0))
in nearest-even mode returns (h,+0). Conversion of binary128 bits
0x3c030000000000001200000000000000 to PPC returns
(binary64 bits 0x0030000000000001,+0). Each constrained operation,
with initial flags zero and exceptions observed, raises exactly inexact
(16). The unconstrained operations return the same bits without
modifying the floating-point environment.
```

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

This changes numerical semantics at an unresolved tie, not carrier types
or helper names. New arith bitcode and manifest hashes must be published
and driver/plugin consumers re-pinned. Materialised device/native closure
bytes, ISA object identities and certificates can consequently change;
do not reuse old result proofs against new linked bodies. The ISA
`ref@0.9` recipe catalog needs no change. A dialect version change also
changes mapping-version bindings. Keep old publication pins reproducible.

## Conformance vectors and actual verification

Ran `timeout 30 python3 -I -B -u
../bennett-arith/tests/ppc_zero_low_witness.py`, with the 1 GiB address-space
bound and headless environment in [INDEX](INDEX.md): exit 0. Exact rational
adjacency and binary128 representability passed. Both LLVM 18 and 23
native libraries returned L with FMA flags 0 and conversion flags 24.
This successful witness run proves the defect; it is not arithmetic
conformance. Sources and native libraries were read-only.

Acceptance vectors must test both witnesses and their negatives, odd/even
highs, low=0/±u, two-zero-low subnormal neighbors, low-subnormal/normal and
high-bin transitions, exact representable values and both signs. Check
nearest-even, nearest-away and all directed modes, sticky unrelated
flags, observed versus ignored exceptions, final-total underflow and
overflow, and every named conversion route reaching PPC. Include a direct
test of the equal-parity fallback. Do not substitute host double arithmetic
for the exact midpoint. Full-domain arithmetic work remains with 4yg.

## K entry and publication route

**No ISA 0.9 or 0.10 K entry is needed:** the ISA executes the linked
integer implementation and does not own PPC value selection. Proposed
dialect decision row: `PPC-ZERO-LOW-PARITY | §13.1 | compare both lows,
including zero; high fallback only for two zero lows; deterministic
same-parity fallback | 4yg.1 | semantic repair`.
Recommend the next published 0.2 patch (0.2.13 if available) and a matching
0.3-draft amendment, through the dialect council. This proposal does not
silently modify 0.2.12 or close 4yg.1. The existing cross-repo request
already routes the decision; no duplicate blocker is needed.

## Mixed fat object

Apply [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md), especially
its separate device-closure and native-container identities (North Star
§§2, 3.3, 4.4). Only marked modules and their required linked helpers need
the revised PPC bodies and device certificates. The corresponding native
comparison uses those same materialised bodies. An ordinary native host
binary and host-only libc need no PPC certificate inventory. No launch,
handle, budget or tape format changes are introduced; the shared note's
container/root association and mixed-launch obligations remain explicit.

I’ll check the cited clauses in both dialect tracks, review the witness and publication rules, and grade the proposed repair. I’m using the repository’s Beads skill for the issue context.

---
name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. Trigger when the user asks to find ready work, claim or close tasks, create follow-up work, inspect blockers, recover project context, or choose between local planning and persistent project tracking.
---

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# Agent Instructions

This project uses **bd** (beads) for issue tracking. Run `bd prime` for full workflow context.

> **Architecture in one line:** Issues live in a local Dolt database
> (`.beads/dolt/`); cross-machine sync uses `bd dolt push/pull` (a
> git-compatible protocol), stored under `refs/dolt/data` on your git
> remote — separate from `refs/heads/*` where your code lives.
> `.beads/issues.jsonl` is a passive export, not the wire protocol.
>
> See [sync-concepts](https://github.com/gastownhall/beads/blob/main/docs/core-concepts/sync-concepts.md)
> for the one-screen overview and anti-patterns (don't treat JSONL as the
> source of truth; don't `bd import` during normal operation; don't
> reach for third-party Dolt hosting before trying the default).

## Quick Reference

```bash
bd ready              # Find available work
bd show <id>          # View issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim work atomically
bd close <id>         # Complete work
bd dolt push          # Push beads data to remote
```

## Non-Interactive Shell Commands

**ALWAYS use non-interactive flags** with file operations to avoid hanging on confirmation prompts.

Shell commands like `cp`, `mv`, and `rm` may be aliased to include `-i` (interactive) mode on some systems, causing the agent to hang indefinitely waiting for y/n input.

**Use these forms instead:**
```bash
# Force overwrite without prompting
cp -f source dest           # NOT: cp source dest
mv -f source dest           # NOT: mv source dest
rm -f file                  # NOT: rm file

# For recursive operations
rm -rf directory            # NOT: rm -r directory
cp -rf source dest          # NOT: cp -r source dest
```

**Other commands that may prompt:**
- `scp` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` for non-interactive
- `ssh` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` to fail instead of prompting
- `apt-get` - use `-y` flag
- `brew` - use `HOMEBREW_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1` env var

## Beads Issue Tracker

This project uses **bd (beads)** for issue tracking. Run `bd prime` to see full workflow context and commands.

### Quick Reference

```bash
bd ready              # Find available work
bd show <id>          # View issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim work
bd close <id>         # Complete work
```

### Rules

- Use `bd` for ALL task tracking — do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown TODO lists
- Run `bd prime` for detailed command reference and session close protocol
- Use `bd remember` for persistent knowledge — do NOT use MEMORY.md files

**Architecture in one line:** issues live in a local Dolt DB; sync uses `refs/dolt/data` on your git remote; `.beads/issues.jsonl` is a passive export. See https://github.com/gastownhall/beads/blob/main/docs/core-concepts/sync-concepts.md for details and anti-patterns.

## Agent Context Profiles

The managed Beads block is task-tracking guidance, not permission to override repository, user, or orchestrator instructions.

- **Conservative (default)**: Use `bd` for task tracking. Do not run git commits, git pushes, or Dolt remote sync unless explicitly asked. At handoff, report changed files, validation, and suggested next commands.
- **Minimal**: Keep tool instruction files as pointers to `bd prime`; use the same conservative git policy unless active instructions say otherwise.
- **Team-maintainer**: Only when the repository explicitly opts in, agents may close beads, run quality gates, commit, and push as part of session close. A current "do not commit" or "do not push" instruction still wins.

## Session Completion

This protocol applies when ending a Beads implementation workflow. It is subordinate to explicit user, repository, and orchestrator instructions.

1. **File issues for remaining work** - Create beads for anything that needs follow-up
2. **Run quality gates** (if code changed) - Tests, linters, builds
3. **Update issue status** - Close finished work, update in-progress items
4. **Handle git/sync by active profile**:
   ```bash
   # Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval.
   git status

   # Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
   git pull --rebase
   bd dolt push
   git push
   git status
   ```
5. **Hand off** - Summarize changes, validation, issue status, and any blocked sync/commit/push step

**Critical rules:**
- Explicit user or orchestrator instructions override this Beads block.
- Do not commit or push without clear authority from the active profile or the current user request.
- If a required sync or push is blocked, stop and report the exact command and error.

# spec/

`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md` is draft **0.2.12** (2026-11-01), from eight consumer-named gaps,
awaiting council review. It supersedes accepted draft 0.2.11 (round 10, sol/codex);
accepted 0.2.10 is retained in git, with binding interpretations in
[`errata-0.2.10.md`](errata-0.2.10.md). Binding interpretations of 0.2.12 itself (text and SHA
unchanged) are in [`errata-0.2.12.md`](errata-0.2.12.md). The reviewer disposition table is in
[`../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md`](../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md).
It targets
ISA 0.8 (`../bennett-isa-spec/spec/`, settled) and is graded against it. `bennett_markers.h` is the
normative C/C++ header of the marker ABI (section 3.3). `Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.2.PROGRESS.md` is
the writing job's log. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett` meta repo
(`docs/council/`, `tools/council.py`); the rubric is `docs/council/rubric-isa.md`, under which any
narrowing of north-star scope is S0.

Rules for changing it:

- **No narrowing** (CLAUDE.md rule 10). A compile-time rejection needs a row in section 22.1 citing
  the north-star clause or maintainer decision that requires it. A deferral (section 24) postpones
  mechanics only and states the capability it keeps. A capability ISA 0.8 cannot express is a
  blocker (section 25.1) with the exact ISA change and a `blocked:` verdict, never a rejection.
- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (header and section 23); drafts are not
  compatible with each other. The reference objects and hashes of section 16 depend on the ISA
  version string, the lifting map and the layout, so they change with any of them.
- **Checked examples** (section 1.5, process rules P-1 to P-3):
  - P-1: every rule id has an `accept` example, every rejection a `reject` example, every blocker
    a `blocked` example; Appendix B is the index, and a rule without one fails the check.
  - P-2: an example the checker cannot yet execute fails (`checker-unimplemented: <capability>`);
    no example is removed or weakened to make the checker pass; the freeze gate is all green.
  - P-3: executed results come from independent executors (LLVM tools, native binaries, the Rust
    ISA reference interpreter `bennett-isa-ref`); Python computes expected artefacts only.
- Run `python3 scripts/dialect_check_v02.py` before anyone grades this draft; it must exit 0. (For 0.2 the
  checker is being extended against the Rust interpreter in a separate job; until then the 0.2
  examples were executed by the writing job as its progress file records.)
- Add every new example or stated result as a tagged block, so the checker executes it; an
  untagged `llvm` or `isa` block is an error. A `semantics=` example must be byte-equal to its
  `corpus/legality/cases/spec-*.ll` file; run `python3 corpus/legality/harness/run.py baseline
  --majors 18,20,23 --cases 'cases/spec-*'` (headless, CLAUDE.md rule 11) after changing one.
- Write derivations out before stating numbers; every stated example result must be computed.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every time it was tried (ISA
  rounds 1-8).
- Revisions go back through the council.
# Binding interpretations of dialect 0.2.12

Published 2026-11-02. Text of draft 0.2.12 at `c775e6d458006fde143f2bf8ec62f07b034fb431`, spec SHA-256 `cfcce70e84ead8a05924c0921f13fc9cb477fdd71f5448c7f6f357ea91349e83`, is unchanged; the full version string, map 0.2.9 and consumer pins remain valid. These interpretations clarify that text without changing any reference result. Draft 0.3 carries the same text in §§5.1, 5.2 and 24.

## 5u3 — §§5.1, 5.2, 24 (council run `contract-5u3-2026-11-02`, round 1 sol and codex accept; `accept-4ht-recheck-2026-11-02` round 1 PASS; muse accept in `accept-muse-batch-2026-11-02`; bead `b-dialect-spec-5u3`)

**§5.1, after K.** The reference bounds INT atoms as above; it imposes no additional
cardinality bound on pointer-offset sets or memory intervals. Implementers
MAY use exact symbolic sets, shared interval relations and SCC
acceleration instead of enumerating represented offsets or slots.
All reference sets and byte/representation-width distinctions must be
preserved for verdicts, candidate sets and dispatch domains. A coarser
summary is not the reference result merely because it is sound for
aliasing. An unfinished analysis reports an unfinished build with the
rule and exhausted implementation resource, not a source rejection,
completed verdict or run-time fault for a valid input.

**§5.2, after the least-fixpoint paragraph.** In a pure modular-translation inclusion SCC, if every cycle transfer is
an unfiltered fixed displacement at one bit width W and preserves one
base/provenance coordinate, close the generated displacements as a
subgroup of Z/(2^W). For a single self-cycle of stride s, each seed a
denotes the coset a modulo gcd(s,2^W). Union distinct seed cosets. For
multiple vertices, retain vertex-specific path displacements; do not
merge their seed coordinates. For edges u→v labelled d_uv,
choose root-to-vertex path displacements p_v and let
g=gcd(2^W,{p_u+d_uv−p_v}). A seed a at u contributes
{a−p_u+p_v+kg mod 2^W} at v. Union contributions from all seeds.
Apply the same rule to a memory relation
only when every transfer preserves the complete storage interval and
representation width and all PT6–PT8 read/overlap results are identical
to the represented individual slots. Retain every ROOT and FN alternative
explicitly. Otherwise use the original rules or another exact method.
PT2's pointer-index width and preserved high pointer bits still apply;
this rule does not replace them by unrestricted full-pointer addition.

**§24.** Analysis representation/acceleration is implementation policy; the
default result is the exact §5 least fixpoint, including existing K=4096
INT abstraction. Time or memory caps do not alter that result. A report
of unfinished analysis keeps the remaining implementation obligation open.
# Binding interpretations of accepted dialect 0.2.10

Published with draft 0.2.12, 2026-11-01. Accepted text at `023f5a771a814270634db3be9cfde88ded69f975`, spec SHA-256 `d4f3c1a88391cc147f8c8fa4f80f82852e3ebe6f880610fc16cd5151f33d7206`, is unchanged; consumer pins remain valid. These interpretations resolve ambiguity in that text; new 0.2.12 ABIs/accuracy requirements are identified separately and are not retroactive ABI changes.

## mkb — §§5.2, 7.2, 12.2 N0

**N0 and resolution order.** Before testing the forwarding-thunk predicate or freezing candidates, the plugin MUST apply the N0 passes of §12.2 to the virtual device bodies used by S2; §7.2 resolution then uses those bodies, and §7.3 hashes their materialised closures.

The plugin MUST retain A(S) for every original source function or alias whose address reaches the region operand, dispatching that address to its post-N0 resolved candidate even when N0 removes the forwarding call; a declaration remaining after N0 is still F(8), but an intrinsic call removed by N0 MUST NOT turn its containing defined body into an external candidate.

## jjr — §§16.9, 17.1

The thin malloc body in §16.9 is a specialised witness whose calls all request 16 bytes. ISA allocation itself is exactly byte-bumping, not aligned; C malloc still owes its target ABI alignment. The general wrapper rounds its requested extent and inserts explicit charged padding allocations when an intervening runtime primitive leaves the next address unaligned, identically in both builds. This resolves the example-versus-C-ABI ambiguity without altering ISA §7.1.

## lbq — §§11.2–11.3, 14.7, 19.2

**Console syscall framing.** For virtual stdout/stderr, runtimes MUST implement `writev` by validating the vector count and total length before the first effect, then emitting one `console.write` per nonempty vector in source order (zero-length writes emit no record), returning the total byte count; the descriptor ABI limits are 0..1024 vectors and total at most target SSIZE_MAX, with invalid counts/overflow returning EINVAL and a null nonempty vector table returning EFAULT.

The console shim MUST return ENOTTY for `ioctl(TIOCGWINSZ)`, ESPIPE for seeking, and success for closing a virtual stdout/stderr descriptor, with no extra device effect or terminal metadata; this close releases no host resource and does not disable the virtual byte sink.

**Model history.** R console writes MUST use only the ISA effect tape, while L model state is ordinary reversible memory initialised from descriptor bindings, private and discarding only under the oracle rules of §11.3; action console capacity exhaustion MUST fault before mutation under TDL-12, and a standalone native-model test undo log MUST NOT substitute for either tier's history. No second production model-history ABI is required.

**Process context.** Before entering `<sym>`, the driver/runtimes preparation MUST establish a static single-thread musl process context with the supplied argc/argv/envp bytes, a null-terminated argv and envp, `environ`, program name, initial fenv and the libc/stdio state needed by the linked program; comparison-only Linux `_start` is permitted, but Linux ELF crt/TLS/auxv entry is not required in device tiers.

For a C `main` entry, the runtimes entry wrapper MUST treat normal return as `exit(status)`, running registered exit handlers and flushing stdio exactly once under §14.7 before the launch stub catches the exit pseudo-exception; explicit `exit` follows that same path, and `_Exit` bypasses handlers/flush as §14.7 specifies.

## 0na — §§3.1, 17.7, 19.5

The “nobody: primitives” ownership row applies to production bitcode before ISA lowering, not native comparison support implementing those primitives after materialisation. Such support must preserve device layout and history. Draft 0.2.12 §19.5 publishes the new v1 layout transport ABI; that spelling is not imposed retroactively on 0.2.10. The existing memory/fault outcomes and North Star definedness classifiers are not waived by UBSan-only support.

## dfd — §§13.1, 13.5

The explicit exact-conversion and correct-rounding guarantees take priority over APFloat implementation fallbacks. PPCDoubleDouble denotes high+low, not the legacy consecutive-106-bit approximation. A low component may not be discarded when the exact sum is representable in the destination. Sparse-tail loss and intermediate unsigned-low saturation are not permitted by the named-format reference. Draft 0.2.12 §13.1 makes canonical-pair rounding and flags explicit; no claim that the current arith publication already meets these guarantees is made.

## 1av — §§13.4–13.5

“No correct-rounding claim” does not authorise an arbitrary deterministic non-numerical substitute for the named operation or waive the specified IEEE flags. The accepted text does not quantify its numerical error. The one-local-ulp carrier bound and precise constrained accuracy/flag policy in draft 0.2.12 are NEW requirements, not retroactive assertions that 0.2.10 contained a numeric bound or that measured sweeps prove a full-domain bound.

## z7u — §13.1 arbitrary-format row

“Format named by metadata” means the LLVM major’s named interpretation MDString and its format/carrier semantics, not an unbounded user-created APFloat precision/exponent schema. LLVM 23 Intrinsics.td and LangRef define to(value, format, rounding, saturation) and from(bits, format). Draft 0.2.12 publishes NEW concrete carrier operand spellings and a static request/manifest protocol; those new ABI details are not claimed to have existed in 0.2.10.

## 21a — §§13.1, 13.5

The generic “soft-float call of §13.1 with suffix _rm” does not merge the distinct LLVM quiet fcmp and signaling fcmps operations. Quiet-NaN invalid behavior must remain distinguishable. Draft 0.2.12 publishes the NEW cmps_B_rm spelling and exact operand/exception lowering in §13.5; the absence of an earlier spelling was a contract gap, not authority to silently substitute quiet comparison.

None is an excuse to reduce a format/width domain, omit an inverse

## Publication and combined decisions

[spec/README.md](../../../spec/README.md) requires a full version change
for semantic changes and council review. “0.9 K repair” in these notes
means a proposed use of the existing council repair process, **not
authority to silently amend a published contract**. Without explicit
in-version authorization, the 2qc/sqr semantic additions go to 0.10.
New version prefixes change affected object/interface/closure envelopes,
example identities and consumer pins. A same-version repair with unchanged
source bytes preserves their source identities but still invalidates
incompatible execution/schedule/proof assumptions. `ref@0.9` changes only
if its own catalog bytes/version change; none of these proposals requires
such a rewrite. Assign numeric K entries only at acceptance.

PPC parity and runtime delivery belong to the dialect because they define
LLVM numerical/helper lowering and build inputs. ISA 0.9/0.10 need no
new K for either. Publish the exact proposed dialect text in the next
available maintained patch and mirror it in 0.3; the proposal is not a
private runtime/driver agreement. The new helper protocol is itself
versioned with schema 1, independent of ISA subformat numbers.

For 2qc use the existing concrete invocation-context vocabulary, owner
closure selection and owner-local claim indices. Coordinate with
[pass2 0jq](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-0jq.md); do not create a second
inverse proof owner or replace tuple applicability by root budget equality.
For sqr retain the written source identity and resolve complete wire
operands before K78 primitive matching and scheduling. A parenthesized
indexed list is not currently a legal substitute under §16.2.

## Mixed fat object

[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md), binding under
bennett-4ht and advisor 08:45Z (North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). A native host
binary remains native; only marked device modules and their required
linked closures become ISA objects. Helper generation, comparison,
certificate inventory and invocation coverage bind those device roots,
with a separate root-relative context [] per root. They do not inventory
native main, host-only argv/envp setup or host-only libc. The shared note
names the exact remaining container/root/certificate association, launch,
budget/handle, tape/run and host-memory mapping changes. This pass neither
implements those mechanisms nor locks them out.

Run the following shell block after the environment prefix. It extracts

def pow2(n):
    return F(2**n) if n >= 0 else F(1, 2**(-n))
def decode(bits, fraction_bits, exponent_bits, bias):
    sign = -1 if bits >> (fraction_bits+exponent_bits) else 1
    exp = (bits >> fraction_bits) & ((1 << exponent_bits)-1)
    frac = bits & ((1 << fraction_bits)-1)
    assert exp != (1 << exponent_bits)-1
    sig = frac if exp == 0 else (1 << fraction_bits) | frac
    return sign * sig * pow2((1-bias if exp == 0 else exp-bias)-fraction_bits)
def f64(bits):
    return decode(bits, 52, 11, 1023)
u = pow2(-1074)
h = f64(0x3ff0000000000001)
assert h == 1+pow2(-52)
exact = (h+u)*F(1,2) + f64(0x3fe0000000000001)
assert exact == h+u/2
H = f64(0x0030000000000001)
V = decode(0x3c030000000000001200000000000000, 112, 15, 16383)
assert V == H+u/2 and H > pow2(-1022)
for hi, v in ((h, exact), (H, V)):
    assert (hi/u).denominator == 1
    assert v-hi == hi+u-v == u/2
    assert hi+u-hi == u
# The two high raw significands and the nonzero low significand are odd.
assert (0x3ff0000000000001 & 1) == (0x0030000000000001 & 1) == 1
assert (0 & 1, 1 & 1) == (0, 1)  # proposed common low comparison selects zero
# Both-low-zero subnormal neighbours still use high parity.
assert f64(2)-f64(1) == u and (1 & 1, 2 & 1) == (1, 0)
print('PASS: independent exact PPC FMA/conversion midpoints and parity distinction')

spec = (ROOT / 'spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md').read_text()
block = spec.split('**CLAIM-REPLAY witness**', 1)[1].split('```json\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
assert block.count('"borrowed":["1"]') == 6
fixed = block.replace('"borrowed":["1"]', '"borrowed":[]')
fixed = fixed.replace('"borrowed":[1]', '"borrowed":[]')

          k13['wrong_row']['before'], k13['wrong_row']['after'],
          k13['changed_arg_after'], k13['short_budget_after']]

assert k13['sample_count'] == 4 and k13['distinct_samples'] == 1
print('PASS: all six K13 tuple repairs; 24-bit domain and exact singleton coverage')

# A finite coverage counterexample, not an implementation of the checker.
minimum = (3,0,0,0)
def covers(tk, arg, domain=range(4), token_arg=None):
    return (all(a <= b for a,b in zip(minimum,tk)) and arg in domain
            and (token_arg is None or token_arg == (3,0,0,0)))

    assert result['symbols']['a']['interface_hash'] == new
    assert result['symbols']['a']['closure_hash'] == new

    assert result['mut'] == [q ^ int(c==3),p] and result['in'] == [c]

## Remaining decisions and ownership

Council/editor owners must ratify and publish these changes; the existing
five issues remain their tracking records. Arith still owes exact incoming
conversion/FMA mechanics and flags after the parity decision. Runtimes
and driver/plugin still owe the helper protocol and bodies. Checker and
executor owners must add the proposed context/operand vectors, and the
K13 editor must publish a complete corrected fixture. None of that
downstream implementation is claimed complete by this notes-only job.
"""Exact witness for the 0.2.12 zero-low tie-parity question.

A successful exit verifies the witness, not arithmetic conformance. All expected
values use Fraction/integer arithmetic. The native result is evidence only.
"""
import importlib.util
from pathlib import Path
import sys

sys.dont_write_bytecode=True
ROOT=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
spec=importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('edge',ROOT/'tests/test_edge.py')
e=importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec);spec.loader.exec_module(e)
f=e.FORMATS['f64'];mask=(1<<64)-1

def value(pair):
    return e.decode(pair&mask,f)[0]+e.decode(pair>>64,f)[0]

def canonical(pair):
    high=pair&mask;low=pair>>64
    h=e.decode(high,f)[0];v=value(pair)
    return e.encode(v,f)[0]==high and e.decode(low,f)[0]==v-h and (low!=1<<63)

hbits=0x3ff0000000000001
h=e.decode(hbits,f)[0];u=e.power(-1074)
a=hbits|(1<<64);b=0x3fe0000000000000;c=0x3fe0000000000001
assert all(canonical(p) for p in (a,b,c))
exact=value(a)*value(b)+value(c)
lower=hbits;upper=a
assert canonical(lower) and canonical(upper)
assert exact==h+u/2
assert exact-value(lower)==value(upper)-exact==u/2
# Every finite binary64 is an integer multiple of u, so every pair sum is too.
# Therefore there can be no pair sum strictly between these two neighbours.
assert (value(lower)/u).denominator==(value(upper)/u).denominator==1
assert (value(upper)-value(lower))/u==1
parity=lambda p:((p>>64) if p>>64 else p&mask)&1
assert parity(lower)==parity(upper)==1
print('Exact FMA is h + 2^-1075, h = 1 + 2^-52; its two adjacent canonical neighbours are equidistant.')
print(f'Lower = {lower:032x}; upper = {upper:032x}; literal zero-low fallback gives odd/odd parity.')
# The same ambiguity is reachable by a binary128 conversion, independently of
# FMA: scale the odd high component into a small normal binary64 high bin.
chbits=(3<<52)|1;ch=e.decode(chbits,f)[0]
clower=chbits;cupper=chbits|(1<<64);cv=ch+u/2
assert canonical(clower) and canonical(cupper)
assert cv-value(clower)==value(cupper)-cv==u/2
assert parity(clower)==parity(cupper)==1
cbits=e.encode(cv,e.FORMATS['f128'])[0]
assert e.decode(cbits,e.FORMATS['f128'])[0]==cv
assert e.encode(cv,f)[0]==chbits
print(f'Exact binary128 input {cbits:032x} is also a zero-low odd/odd canonical-pair midpoint.')
for major in map(int,sys.argv[1:] or (18,23)):
    edge=e.Edge(major);edge.env[1]=0
    actual=edge.call('fma_ppcf128_rm',a,b,c,0)[0]
    print(f'LLVM {major} current result {actual:032x}, flags={edge.env[1]}; every nearest result must be inexact (16).')
    edge.env[1]=0
    actual=edge.call('fptrunc_f128_ppcf128_rm',cbits,0)[0]
    print(f'LLVM {major} current conversion {actual:032x}, flags={edge.env[1]}; every nearest result must be inexact (16), without underflow.')
print('Witness verified; the nearest-even tie rule requires a contract decision. This is not a conformance pass.')

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6846:The arith distribution MUST provide a documented offline generator accepting that request and producing matching-major ordinary bitcode plus a JCS manifest containing `schema: "bennett-arith-instances-1"`, the request SHA-256 as `request_sha256`, `bitcode_sha256`, and `exports` (array of objects with exact `symbol` and LLVM `function_type` strings sorted by symbol); every requested definition and its reachable closure MUST be present, with no hardware FP or host libm import, and linked before normalisation identically in both builds. Default prebuilt exponent widths are a cache, never a capability bound; generation failure is an explicit implementation blocker, not a successful link, unsupported-format verdict, or silent narrower substitute.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6893:**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6895:For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6897:Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6899:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6818:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6824:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6867:The arith distribution MUST provide a documented offline generator accepting that request and producing matching-major ordinary bitcode plus a JCS manifest containing `schema: "bennett-arith-instances-1"`, the request SHA-256 as `request_sha256`, `bitcode_sha256`, and `exports` (array of objects with exact `symbol` and LLVM `function_type` strings sorted by symbol); every requested definition and its reachable closure MUST be present, with no hardware FP or host libm import, and linked before normalisation identically in both builds. Default prebuilt exponent widths are a cache, never a capability bound; generation failure is an explicit implementation blocker, not a successful link, unsupported-format verdict, or silent narrower substitute.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6914:**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6916:For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6918:Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6920:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

## Publication and combined decisions

[spec/README.md](../../../spec/README.md) requires a full version change
for semantic changes and council review. “0.9 K repair” in these notes
means a proposed use of the existing council repair process, **not
authority to silently amend a published contract**. Without explicit
in-version authorization, the 2qc/sqr semantic additions go to 0.10.
New version prefixes change affected object/interface/closure envelopes,
example identities and consumer pins. A same-version repair with unchanged
source bytes preserves their source identities but still invalidates
incompatible execution/schedule/proof assumptions. `ref@0.9` changes only
if its own catalog bytes/version change; none of these proposals requires
such a rewrite. Assign numeric K entries only at acceptance.

PPC parity and runtime delivery belong to the dialect because they define
LLVM numerical/helper lowering and build inputs. ISA 0.9/0.10 need no
new K for either. Publish the exact proposed dialect text in the next
available maintained patch and mirror it in 0.3; the proposal is not a
private runtime/driver agreement. The new helper protocol is itself
versioned with schema 1, independent of ISA subformat numbers.

For 2qc use the existing concrete invocation-context vocabulary, owner
closure selection and owner-local claim indices. Coordinate with
[pass2 0jq](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-0jq.md); do not create a second
inverse proof owner or replace tuple applicability by root budget equality.
For sqr retain the written source identity and resolve complete wire
operands before K78 primitive matching and scheduling. A parenthesized
indexed list is not currently a legal substitute under §16.2.

## Mixed fat object

Every note cites and applies
[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md), binding under
bennett-4ht and advisor 08:45Z (North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). A native host
binary remains native; only marked device modules and their required
linked closures become ISA objects. Helper generation, comparison,
certificate inventory and invocation coverage bind those device roots,
with a separate root-relative context [] per root. They do not inventory
native main, host-only argv/envp setup or host-only libc. The shared note
names the exact remaining container/root/certificate association, launch,
budget/handle, tape/run and host-memory mapping changes. This pass neither
implements those mechanisms nor locks them out.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6818:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6824:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6914:**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6916:For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.

## 13. Floating point (arith contract)

NS §3.2: "Floating point is deterministic soft-float in both builds … Hardware floating point
never executes in either build." Every floating-point construct of every format has a row
here; none faults.

### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)

**Formats and carriers:**

| LLVM type | Suffix | Carrier |
|---|---|---|
| `half` | `f16` | `i16` |
| `bfloat` | `bf16` | `i16` |
| `float` | `f32` | `i32` |
| `double` | `f64` | `i64` |
| `x86_fp80` | `f80` | `i80` |
| `fp128` | `f128` | `i128` |
| `ppc_fp128` | `ppcf128` | `i128` |

The rewrite of an instruction `%r` with floating-point operands or result is: first, for each
floating-point operand k (value or constant, in operand order), `%nz.<k>.r = bitcast <T>
<operand> to <carrier>`; then the call below, named `%nz.i.r`; then, for a floating-point result,
`%r = bitcast <carrier> %nz.i.r to <T>`. The entry points are defined by `bennett-arith` and are
part of its bitcode the driver links. B below is the format's suffix, C its carrier.

| Instruction | Call (carrier operands in order) | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `fadd`, `fsub`, `fmul`, `fdiv`, `frem` | `C @__bennett_sf_{add,sub,mul,div,rem}_B(C, C)` | bitcast |
| `fcmp <p>` | `i1 @__bennett_sf_cmp_B(C, C, i32 P)`, P the LLVM predicate number: `false` 0, `oeq` 1, `ogt` 2, `oge` 3, `olt` 4, `ole` 5, `one` 6, `ord` 7, `uno` 8, `ueq` 9, `ugt` 10, `uge` 11, `ult` 12, `ule` 13, `une` 14, `true` 15 | `%r` is the call itself |
| `fptosi`/`fptoui` to iM, `llvm.fptosi.sat`/`llvm.fptoui.sat` to iM | `i128 @__bennett_sf_{fptosi,fptoui}_B(C, i32 M)` | `%r = trunc i128 %nz.i.r to iM` (the call is `%r` when M = 128) |
| `sitofp`/`uitofp` from iM | `%nz.x.r = sext`/`zext iM a to i128` (omitted when M = 128), `C @__bennett_sf_{sitofp,uitofp}_B(i128)` | bitcast |
| `fpext`, `fptrunc` from format S to format D; `llvm.convert.to.fp16`, `llvm.convert.from.fp16` | `D_carrier @__bennett_sf_{fpext,fptrunc}_S_D(S_carrier)` | bitcast |
| `llvm.fptrunc.round(x, mode)` | `D_carrier @__bennett_sf_fptrunc_S_D_rm(S_carrier, i32 R)`, R from the mode (§13.5) | bitcast |
| `llvm.sqrt`, `llvm.floor`, `llvm.ceil`, `llvm.trunc`, `llvm.round`, `llvm.roundeven`, `llvm.rint`, `llvm.nearbyint`, `llvm.canonicalize` | `C @__bennett_sf_<name>_B(C)` | bitcast |
| `llvm.minnum`, `llvm.maxnum`, `llvm.minimum`, `llvm.maximum`, `llvm.minimumnum`, `llvm.maximumnum` | `C @__bennett_sf_<name>_B(C, C)` | bitcast |
| `llvm.fma` | `C @__bennett_sf_fma_B(C, C, C)` | bitcast |
| `llvm.is.fpclass(x, mask)` | `i1 @__bennett_sf_isfpclass_B(C, i32 mask)` | the call |
| `llvm.ldexp(x, e)`, `llvm.frexp(x)` | `C @__bennett_sf_ldexp_B(C, i32 e)`; `{ C, i32 } @__bennett_sf_frexp_B(C)` | bitcast (for frexp: of field 0) |
| `llvm.lround`, `llvm.llround`, `llvm.lrint`, `llvm.llrint` to iM | `i128 @__bennett_sf_{lround,llround,lrint,llrint}_B(C, i32 M)` | `trunc` to iM |
| `llvm.convert.to.arbitrary.fp`, `llvm.convert.from.arbitrary.fp` (format named by metadata) | `@__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B` / `@__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B` with LLVM APFloat's semantics of the named format | bitcast |
| `fneg`, `llvm.fabs`, `llvm.copysign`, `bitcast`, `load`, `store`, `select`, `phi` | unchanged: bit-exact operations (LangRef) | – |

**Arbitrary-format naming and carriers.** Format metadata MUST be the LLVM-major LangRef interpretation MDString, not a user-defined precision/exponent record; the integer carrier is exactly the interpretation's storage width, scalarised lane by lane by N21. LLVM 23's names are `Float8E5M2`, `Float8E5M2FNUZ`, `Float8E4M3`, `Float8E4M3FN`, `Float8E4M3FNUZ`, `Float8E4M3B11FNUZ`, `Float8E3M4`, `Float8E8M0FNU` (i8), `Float6E3M2FN`, `Float6E2M3FN` (i6), and `Float4E2M1FN` (i4). These intrinsics are absent on LLVM 18; the name set is the major's published set, not a list of formats that its current APFloat converter happens to implement.

For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.

**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).

The arith distribution MUST provide a documented offline generator accepting that request and producing matching-major ordinary bitcode plus a JCS manifest containing `schema: "bennett-arith-instances-1"`, the request SHA-256 as `request_sha256`, `bitcode_sha256`, and `exports` (array of objects with exact `symbol` and LLVM `function_type` strings sorted by symbol); every requested definition and its reachable closure MUST be present, with no hardware FP or host libm import, and linked before normalisation identically in both builds. Default prebuilt exponent widths are a cache, never a capability bound; generation failure is an explicit implementation blocker, not a successful link, unsupported-format verdict, or silent narrower substitute.

**Wide integer sides (M>128).** The preceding i128 conversion rows apply only
to M≤128. For fptosi/fptoui, their .sat forms and lround/llround/lrint/llrint,
N32 emits `void @__bennett_sf_<op>_B_wide(ptr d, C operand, i32 M)`.
Here d is a shadow-stack slot of L=ceil(M/64) limbs at byte offsets 8k (§12.7).
The entry writes the exact M-bit saturated result, limb 0 least significant,
using truncation toward zero / the rounding rule above, with NaN mapped to 0;
N22 loads each limb at width min(64,M−64k). For sitofp/uitofp from iM, store
all limbs in slot s and call `C @__bennett_sf_<op>_B_wide(ptr s, i32 M)`,
correctly rounded from the exact signed/unsigned M-bit integer. No i128 carrier
intervenes. For canonical temporary slots, emit ss_push(8L,8), then limb
GEP/store rows in increasing k; for destinations, push then call then limb
GEP/load rows in increasing k; pop slots in reverse push order after their
last use. Bitcast FP operands before destination push and FP results after pop.
The usual shadow-stack checks, budget charges and linked-runtime materialisation
apply. The helper's suffix op is fptosi/fptoui for the corresponding .sat form.

**Semantics of the entry points** (the contract with `bennett-arith`): IEEE 754-2019 for
binary16 (`half`), binary32, binary64 and binary128; `bfloat` is the IEEE-style format with 8
exponent and 7 fraction bits; `x86_fp80` is the 80-bit x87 extended format (64-bit significand
with explicit integer bit; encodings with exponent ≠ 0 and integer bit 0, the unnormals, and
pseudo-denormals and pseudo-infinities are treated as NaN inputs, as modern x87 hardware does);
`ppc_fp128` is double-double with the operations of LLVM's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` semantics of
the major in use. For all formats: round to nearest ties to even, subnormals supported, no
observable flags or exceptions (except §13.5); **every NaN result is the format's canonical NaN**
(sign 0, quiet bit set, other payload bits 0: `0x7E00` f16, `0x7FC0` bf16, `0x7FC00000` f32,
`0x7FF8000000000000` f64, `0x7FFFC000000000000000` f80, `0x7FFF8000…0` f128, and for ppcf128
the f64 canonical NaN in the high double and +0 in the low); `rem` is C `fmod` (exact, the sign of
the dividend); `cmp` is LLVM's `fcmp` for the predicate; `fptosi`/`fptoui` and the `.sat` forms
truncate toward zero, map NaN to 0 and saturate values outside the range of iM to its bounds,
returned sign-extended (`fptosi`) or zero-extended (`fptoui`) in i128 when M≤128,
and in the full limb slot when M>128; `sitofp`/`uitofp` round to
nearest even; `minnum`/`maxnum` are IEEE 754-2008 minNum/maxNum (one NaN operand: the other
operand; two: the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimum`/`maximum` are IEEE 754-2019 (either NaN:
the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimumnum`/`maximumnum` are IEEE 754-2019 minimumNumber/
maximumNumber; `sqrt`, `fma` and every rounding function are correctly rounded (`round` ties
away from zero, `rint` and `nearbyint` to nearest even); `fpext` is exact; `fptrunc` rounds to
nearest even; `canonicalize` maps every NaN to the canonical NaN and is the identity otherwise;
`is.fpclass` tests LangRef's ten classes on the bit pattern; `ldexp`/`frexp` are exact up to
rounding of a subnormal `ldexp` result; `lround`/`llround` round half away from zero and `lrint`/
`llrint` to nearest even, saturating like `fptosi`. *(non-normative)* `fneg`, `fabs` and
`copysign` stay bit-exact, so a NaN's sign bit set by `fneg` survives, as LangRef requires; an
implementation of `half` and `bfloat` + − × ÷ √ in binary32 followed by one rounding is
correctly rounded, since 24 ≥ 2p + 2 for p = 11 and p = 8 (Figueroa's double-rounding bound), but
the contract is correct rounding, so `fma` needs its own implementation.

**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.

For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.

Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.

Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.

### 13.2 Vector floating point

After N21 every vector floating-point operation is scalar: §13.1 applies lane by lane, and
`vector.reduce.fadd`/`fmul` are the ordered left folds of §12.6 (exact once N2 has removed
`reassoc`).

### 13.3 Formats beyond binary32 and binary64

All seven formats of §13.1 are required by the same table and contract (NS §3.2). A producer MUST report a route as `lowered` only when its linked implementation satisfies that complete contract; a missing numerical implementation is an implementation blocker, never permission to reject, trap or silently narrow the format capability. musl's
`fmt_fp` (`printf("%Lf")`, rung 3) uses `x86_fp80` on x86-64 and `fp128` on AArch64 and RISC-V64.

### 13.4 Transcendentals and libm intrinsics (N33)

`llvm.sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `asin`, `acos`, `atan`, `atan2`, `sinh`, `cosh`, `tanh`, `exp`, `exp2`,
`exp10`, `log`, `log2`, `log10`, `pow`, `powi`, `sincos`, `sincospi` and `modf` become calls of
the **runtimes libm**: `@<name>` for `double`, `@<name>f` for `float`,
and `@<name>l` for the target's native `long double` format D (x86-64:
`x86_fp80`; AArch64 and RISC-V64: `fp128`). For every other source format B,
including half, bfloat and non-native x86_fp80/fp128/ppc_fp128, use a direct
own-format carrier call `C_B @__bennett_sf_<name>_<suffix>(C_B, …)`;
C_B and suffix are exactly §13.1's table. No conversion through D is emitted.
All seven source formats remain covered on every supported platform; a format
different from D is neither rejected nor trapped. This route preserves the
source format's precision and cannot request a narrowing fpext or an equal-size
cross-format cast.

For these carrier calls, bitcast each floating operand k to its **own** carrier
in operand order as `%nz.<k>.r`, call as `%nz.i.r`, and bitcast the scalar
floating result back as `%r` (§13.1). Retain non-floating operands and their types
in source order. These linked bennett-arith/runtimes functions implement the
named operation numerically in B, using the same deterministic, versioned
implementation in both builds; they are not bit reinterpretations between FP
formats and do not use the host C long-double ABI. NaN results use §13.1's
canonical encoding, with signed zeros preserved where the operation requires.
As for the native-format libm below, no correct-rounding claim is made.

`powi(x,n)` retains repeated-squaring semantics (compiler-rt's `__powidf2`),
with the integer exponent unconverted; its carrier spelling is
`C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<suffix>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` at the intrinsic's exponent width K
(the floating-ABI spelling remains `__bennett_rt_powi_B`). `sincos(x)` is the
pair (sin x, cos x), `sincospi(x)` the pair (sinpi x, cospi x), and `modf(x)`
the pair (fractional, integral). Their carrier entry points return `{ C_B, C_B }`.
For powi and these pair operations use this carrier route on every format,
avoiding the C pointer-output ABI. Reconstruct pair fields in order j=0,1:
`%nz.outbits<j>.r = extractvalue` of `%nz.i.r`, then bitcast to B as
`%nz.outfp<j>.r`, then insert into `{ B, B }` (first into zeroinitializer as
`%nz.out0.r`, finally as `%r`). Thus the special operations retain their
semantics and field order under the same format policy.

The runtimes libm is musl-derived and compiled through this pipeline, so it is
soft-float code itself and the same function runs in both builds; calls of
`sin`, `exp`, … in source resolve to it by §4.5. The certificate records the
runtimes/arith versions. No correct-rounding claim is required; bit-exact
determinism is (NS §3.2). The carrier routines are linked bitcode definitions,
normalised by this same pipeline, not new ISA primitives or unresolved host calls.

**Accuracy envelope.** For each finite mathematical result of a §13.4 own-format carrier transcendental, the implementation MUST return a B value within one local ulp of that exact result, over the entire finite input domain; local ulp is the spacing of B's adjacent finite values bracketing the exact result, taking an exactly representable result as the lower endpoint (the upper endpoint at the largest finite value), with the least positive subnormal spacing at zero or in the subnormal interval. For ppcf128 use §13.1's ordered canonical-pair values. Special values, domain errors, poles, signed zeros and overflow results follow the named operation's mathematical/IEEE semantics rather than this finite-result envelope. At overflow the result is the correctly rounded overflow outcome for the requested mode. This is an error bound, not a general correct-rounding claim.

The bound MUST apply separately to both fields of sincos/sincospi; modf MUST split the input exactly with truncation toward zero and preserve required zero signs, and powi MUST retain its specified repeated-squaring sequence with each multiplication/division rounded under §13.1, rather than claiming the one-ulp bound for the whole sequence.

For `_rm` carrier transcendentals, implementations MUST choose a result within the same envelope on the requested side of the exact result for directed modes, use nearest-side selection for the two nearest modes, and raise IEEE invalid for a domain error or signaling NaN, divbyzero for a pole, overflow when the correctly rounded mathematical result overflows B, underflow when that result is tiny after rounding and inexact, and inexact iff the returned finite value differs from the exact mathematical result; approximation work alone MUST NOT set a flag on an exactly representable mathematical result. For nearest-side selection, compare the distances to the two B neighbours bracketing the exact result, with ties to even or away as requested; this constrained case is correctly rounded. Unconstrained carrier operations retain the one-ulp allowance. Flags for modf/powi follow their exact split/sequenced arithmetic semantics. The native-format musl libm accuracy policy remains the recorded, versioned musl implementation; the quantitative carrier envelope applies to every own-format carrier route.

### 13.5 Constrained intrinsics, `strictfp` and the floating-point environment

bennett-runtimes defines @__bennett_rt_fenv as a global { i32, i32 }, align 4; round is field 0 at byte offset 0, flags is field 1 at byte offset 4, initially {0,0}; bennett-arith declares that same object.

`llvm.experimental.constrained.<op>(…, metadata <rounding>, metadata <except>)` becomes the
soft-float call of §13.1 with the suffix `_rm` and an extra `i32 R` operand: R = 0 for
`round.tonearest`, 1 `round.downward`, 2 `round.upward`, 3 `round.towardzero`, 4
`round.tonearestaway`, and for `round.dynamic` the value loaded from the runtimes global
`__bennett_rt_fenv` (field `round`); when `<except>` is not `fpexcept.ignore`, the entry point
ORs the IEEE flags it raises (invalid 1, divbyzero 2, overflow 4, underflow 8, inexact 16) into
`__bennett_rt_fenv` (field `flags`). `llvm.get.rounding`, `set.rounding`, `get.fpenv`,
`set.fpenv`, `reset.fpenv`, `get.fpmode`, `set.fpmode`, `reset.fpmode` read and write that state;
musl's `<fenv.h>` functions in `bennett-runtimes` do the same. Unconstrained operations use round
to nearest and raise no flags, as LLVM assumes of the default environment. A `strictfp` call-site
attribute on a marker is benign (§3.6).

**Quiet and signaling comparison ABI.** N32 MUST lower `llvm.experimental.constrained.fcmp` to `i1 @__bennett_sf_cmp_B_rm(C_B, C_B, i32 P, i32 R)` and `llvm.experimental.constrained.fcmps` to the distinct `i1 @__bennett_sf_cmps_B_rm(C_B, C_B, i32 P, i32 R)`, with P exactly the 0..15 predicate encoding of §13.1 and R = 0 (these intrinsics have exception metadata but no rounding metadata); vector forms scalarise first, and no predicate-bit extension encodes signalingness.

Both entries MUST compute the same LLVM predicate truth value, while quiet `cmp` raises invalid only for a signaling NaN operand and signaling `cmps` raises invalid for either quiet or signaling NaN, even for predicates false/true; neither raises divbyzero, overflow, underflow or inexact, and no comparison canonicalises or modifies its input bits.

For `fpexcept.ignore`, the normaliser MUST use `i1 @__bennett_sf_cmp_B(C_B, C_B, i32 P)` for either intrinsic so the observable fenv is unchanged; for `fpexcept.maytrap` or `fpexcept.strict` it MUST use the corresponding `_rm` entry, OR invalid into the sticky flags and return the predicate without a host trap (the dialect's deterministic exception convention), identically on LLVM 18 and 23. The plain cmp entry therefore has no flag side effect, and every `_rm` comparison entry always records the flags it raises.

All seven formats MUST retain own-format arithmetic and transcendental semantics, with vector operations scalarised first and identical deterministic carrier implementations in the materialised native and lifted builds. [deps: DLT-13.1, DLT-13.2, DLT-13.3, DLT-13.4, DLT-13.5, DLT-12.1-P1, ISA-4.2]

Suite DF checks the independent §§13.1/13.4/13.5 boundary vectors in `scripts/0212_numerical.c`; full-domain accuracy and exact-flag implementation obligations remain open.

6893:**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.
6897:Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.
6899:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part.
